class Solution:
    def deckRevealedIncreasing(self, deck: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        def reveal(n):
            lst = list(range(n))
            ans = []
            i = 0
            while lst:
                if not i&1: ans.append(lst.pop(0))
                else: lst.append(lst.pop(0))
                i += 1
            return ans
        ans = reveal(len(deck))
        ans = sorted([v, i] for i, v in enumerate(ans))
        deck.sort()
        return deck[j] for i,j in ans

class Solution:

            lst = list(range(n))
            ans = []

                i += 1

Reason in a repair-and-recheck way.

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- staying in diagnosis mode for too long without testing the next plausible edit;
- moving from a local repair into a fresh branch of speculative causes when the current path has not been rechecked yet;
- turning a focused fix into a broad rewrite unless the surrounding code clearly forces it.

class Solution:
    def deckRevealedIncreasing(self, deck: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        def reveal(n):
            lst = list(range(n))
            ans = []
            i = 0
            while lst:
                if not i&1: ans.append(lst.pop(0))
                else: lst.append(lst.pop(0))
                i += 1
            return ans
        ans = reveal(len(deck))
        ans = sorted([v, i] for i, v in enumerate(ans))
        deck.sort()
        return deck[j] for i,j in ans

class Solution:
    def deckRevealedIncreasing(self, deck: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        n = len(deck)
        idx = list(range(n))
        order = []
        while idx:
            order.append(idx.pop(0))
            if idx:
                idx.append(idx.pop(0))
        deck.sort()
        ans = [0] * n
        for card, pos in zip(deck, order):
            ans[pos] = card
        return ans

from collections import deque
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def deckRevealedIncreasing(self, deck: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        n = len(deck)
        idx = deque(range(n))
        order = []
        while idx:
            order.append(idx.popleft())
            if idx:
                idx.append(idx.popleft())
        deck.sort()
        ans = [0] * n
        for card, pos in zip(deck, order):
            ans[pos] = card
        return ans

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def deckRevealedIncreasing(self, deck: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        def reveal(n):
            lst = list(range(n))
            ans = []
            i = 0
            while lst:
                if not i & 1:
                    ans.append(lst.pop(0))
                else:
                    lst.append(lst.pop(0))
                i += 1
            return ans

        order = reveal(len(deck))
        deck.sort()
        result = [0] * len(deck)
        for card, pos in zip(deck, order):
            result[pos] = card
        return result

This is a clean fix. But maybe if we want minimal change from original, we can keep sorted pairs and fix return:

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def deckRevealedIncreasing(self, deck: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        def reveal(n):
            lst = list(range(n))
            ans = []
            i = 0
            while lst:
                if not i & 1:
                    ans.append(lst.pop(0))
                else:
                    lst.append(lst.pop(0))
                i += 1
            return ans

        ans = reveal(len(deck))
        ans = sorted([v, i] for i, v in enumerate(ans))
        deck.sort()
        return [deck[j] for i, j in ans]

Original:

```
class Solution:
    def deckRevealedIncreasing(self, deck: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        def reveal(n):
            lst = list(range(n))
            ans = []
            i = 0
            while lst:
                if not i&1: ans.append(lst.pop(0))
                else: lst.append(lst.pop(0))
                i += 1
            return ans
        ans = reveal(len(deck))
        ans = sorted([v, i] for i, v in enumerate(ans))
        deck.sort()
        return deck[j] for i,j in ans
```

Potential issues:
1. `List` not imported. If run standalone, NameError at class definition. Need import.
2. `return deck[j] for i,j in ans` missing brackets. SyntaxError.
3. `ans = sorted([v, i] for i, v in enumerate(ans))` uses variable `ans` overwritten; okay.
4. `deck.sort()` mutates deck; okay.
5. `reveal` uses `lst.pop(0)` O(n^2), but not a bug.
6. `if not i&1` maybe okay.
7. For n=0, `reveal(0)` returns [], sorted([]) = [], deck.sort(), return [] (with brackets) okay.
8. For n=1, reveal(1): i=0 reveal 0, lst=[], ans=[0]; sorted pairs [[0,0]]; deck.sort; return [deck[0]] correct.

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def deckRevealedIncreasing(self, deck: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        def reveal(n):
            lst = list(range(n))
            ans = []
            i = 0
            while lst:
                if not i & 1:
                    ans.append(lst.pop(0))
                else:
                    lst.append(lst.pop(0))
                i += 1
            return ans

        ans = reveal(len(deck))
        ans = sorted([v, i] for i, v in enumerate(ans))
        deck.sort()
        return [deck[j] for i, j in ans]
```

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def deckRevealedIncreasing(self, deck: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        def reveal(n):
            lst = list(range(n))
            ans = []
            i = 0
            while lst:
                if not i & 1:
                    ans.append(lst.pop(0))
                else:
                    lst.append(lst.pop(0))
                i += 1
            return ans

        ans = reveal(len(deck))
        ans = sorted([v, i] for i, v in enumerate(ans))
        deck.sort()
        return [deck[j] for i, j in ans]

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def deckRevealedIncreasing(self, deck: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        n = len(deck)
        idx = list(range(n))
        order = []
        while idx:
            order.append(idx.pop(0))
            if idx:
                idx.append(idx.pop(0))
        deck.sort()
        ans = [0] * n
        for card, pos in zip(deck, order):
            ans[pos] = card
        return ans

Maybe we can improve by using `deque` to avoid O(n^2) and simplify reveal:

```
from collections import deque
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def deckRevealedIncreasing(self, deck: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        n = len(deck)
        idx = deque(range(n))
        order = []
        while idx:
            order.append(idx.popleft())
            if idx:
                idx.append(idx.popleft())
        deck.sort()
        ans = [0] * n
        for card, pos in zip(deck, order):
            ans[pos] = card
        return ans
```

This is a more robust fix. But it rewrites more. The prompt says "repair-and-recheck" and "avoid broad rewrite unless surrounding code clearly forces it." The minimal fix is enough. ```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def deckRevealedIncreasing(self, deck: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        def reveal(n):
            lst = list(range(n))
            ans = []
            i = 0
            while lst:
                if not i & 1:
                    ans.append(lst.pop(0))
                else:
                    lst.append(lst.pop(0))
                i += 1
            return ans

        ans = reveal(len(deck))
        ans = sorted([v, i] for i, v in enumerate(ans))
        deck.sort()
        return [deck[j] for _, j in ans]
```

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def deckRevealedIncreasing(self, deck: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        def reveal(n):
            lst = list(range(n))
            ans = []
            i = 0
            while lst:
                if not i & 1:
                    ans.append(lst.pop(0))
                else:
                    lst.append(lst.pop(0))
                i += 1
            return ans

        ans = reveal(len(deck))
        ans = sorted([v, i] for i, v in enumerate(ans))
        deck.sort()
        return [deck[j] for _, j in ans]
```

The final code can be:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def deckRevealedIncreasing(self, deck: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        def reveal(n):
            lst = list(range(n))
            ans = []
            i = 0
            while lst:
                if i % 2 == 0:
                    ans.append(lst.pop(0))
                else:
                    lst.append(lst.pop(0))
                i += 1
            return ans

        ans = reveal(len(deck))
        ans = sorted([v, i] for i, v in enumerate(ans))
        deck.sort()
        return [deck[j] for _, j in ans]
```

=== RAW ANSWER ===
as code only.</think>```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def deckRevealedIncreasing(self, deck: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        def reveal(n):
            lst = list(range(n))
            ans = []
            i = 0
            while lst:
                if not i & 1:
                    ans.append(lst.pop(0))
                else:
                    lst.append(lst.pop(0))
                i += 1
            return ans

        ans = reveal(len(deck))
        ans = sorted([v, i] for i, v in enumerate(ans))
        deck.sort()
        return [deck[j] for _, j in ans]
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def deckRevealedIncreasing(self, deck: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        def reveal(n):
            lst = list(range(n))
            ans = []
            i = 0
            while lst:
                if not i & 1:
                    ans.append(lst.pop(0))
                else:
                    lst.append(lst.pop(0))
                i += 1
            return ans

        ans = reveal(len(deck))
        ans = sorted([v, i] for i, v in enumerate(ans))
        deck.sort()
        return [deck[j] for _, j in ans]
